ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-10.u72	Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members! ♪ I can't believe of the Committee on Budget and Finance on the State Budget Bill of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022, No 47-202-01-7, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 1 February 2022. At a meeting held on 4 February 2022, the Budget and Finance Committee examined the State Budget Bill of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022. The meeting was attended by Asen Vassilev Deputy Prime Minister of EU Funds and Minister of Finance; Dimitar Radev Executive Director of BNB; Tsvetan Tsvetkov Chairman of the Court of Auditors; Prof. Tsvetan Tsvetkov Boris Grozdanov, Chairman of the Fiscal Council; the Ombudsman of the Republic of Bulgaria Diana Kovacheva, representatives of trade unions and employers' organizations and others. Minister Vassilev presented the State Budget Bill of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022 and stressed that in it and in the updated medium-term budget forecast for the period 2022 the basic policies and assumptions, as well as the effects of certain priority measures, as set out in the Joint Management Agreement of the Republic of Bulgaria in the period 2021, are reflected. In view of the need to refine and value part of the policies and reforms in the sectors related to the forthcoming adoption of the government's programme for the mandate of its management, it is intended to be laid down in a subsequent update of the budget, which, however, will not affect the objectives of the budgetary balance. I. Macroeconomic forecast for the period 2022 - 2024 Main macroeconomic indicators: 1. Gross domestic product. The macroeconomic outlook for the forecast period is to accelerate GDP growth to 4.8% in 2022, driven by investments. Public investment will be supported by the funds under the National Recovery and Sustainability Plan. In 2023 and 2024, economic growth is expected to slow down to 3,7% and 3,4%, respectively. 2. Labor market. For 2022 a relatively high increase in the number of employees of 1% is expected, which will be determined at the same time by improving the epidemiological situation in the world and in particular in Bulgaria, as well as by the positive effect of the first financial flows under the National Recovery and Sustainability Plan. As a result of the positive dynamics of employment, the unemployment rate for 2022 is expected to decrease to 5%. During the period 2023, the growth of employment will remain high (in the range of 0,8.2%), influenced by the expected relatively high growth of the Bulgarian economy and the unemployment rate will continue its trend at a reduction and is expected to reach an annual average of 4.3% in 2024. 3. Inflation. The average annual inflation increase harmonised indices of consumer prices in 2022 is forecast to be 5.6% and inflation at the end of the year will slow to 3.1%. In the last two years of the forecast period, we expect the average annual inflation to be around 2.7% in 2023 and 2.2% in 2024. II. Financial policy and basic parameters for the period 2022 The revenue for the period 2022 - 2024, expressed as a percentage of GDP, ranged from 40% to 40,2% and, to a nominal amount, increased by 57,253,3 million. lv. For 2022, it is expected to reach 64 537.9 million. lv. in 2024, expenditure, expressed as a percentage of GDP, showed a trend of a decrease of 44,2% in 2022 to 43,2% in 2024. In nominal amounts, expenditure increased by 63 153,8 million. lv. For 2022, it is expected to reach 69 402.8 million. lv. in 2024, in the medium term, expenditure on the consolidated fiscal programme (excluding expenditure incurred by accounts of EU funds and other international programmes and contracts equivalent thereto, including the related national co-financing) is within the 40 per cent rule of the Public Finance Act, with a decrease of 40,0% of GDP in 2022 to 37,1% of GDP in 2024. The deficit under the consolidated fiscal programme on a cash-based basis is expected to reach 4.1% of GDP in 2022 with included COVID-19 measures and 2,5% of GDP without COVID-19 measures with subsequent annual fiscal consolidation steps, reducing to 3,0% of GDP in 2024. From the perspective of the fiscal deficit rule on the consolidated fiscal programme for the entire forecast period, the budget balance is within the deficit limit of up to 3 per cent of GDP. A consolidation of the sector's deficit is envisaged for the government-based sector at the end of the period, with a negative budget balance falling over the period 2022. In 2021, the budget balance is expected to be negative at 4,0% of GDP. A deficit of 5,5% of GDP for 2022, 3,6% for 2023 and 2,8% of GDP for 2024 is expected in the medium term. If the impact of temporary income and cost measures related to the prevention of the spread of COVID-19 in the country and the impact of the crisis is taken into account, the negative balance of the current year is expected to be 3,3% and 3,5% for 2023, respectively. The minimum amount of the fiscal reserve as of 31.12.2022 is intended to remain unchanged compared to the investments for 2021 amounting to 4,5 billion. lv. On the basis of the assumptions and estimated net debt financing in 2022, the government debt is expected to reach a level of 47,1 billion at the end of the period. BGN and the ratio of government debt to GDP to 29.3%, a smooth extension of the average remaining debt maturity, as well as an increase of the share of domestic government debt in the debt structure from 20.5% at the end of 2020 to 34.6% at the end of 2024. III. Under the state budget of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022 For the period 2022, the planned tax policy will continue to adhere to the established principles and guidelines in recent years and its main objectives are once again aimed at maintaining economic growth, improving the business environment, combating tax abuse and increasing fiscal sustainability in the long term. It is envisaged to maintain low tax rates for corporate taxes and income taxes for individuals as an important prerequisite for investment, economic growth and employment. The estimated tax revenue for the period 2022 " 2024' is based on the main indicators of the macroeconomic forecast of the Ministry of Finance for the period 2021 " 2024' and on the results of the measures implemented by the revenue agencies to improve collection. With the Draft Law on the State Budget of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022 it is proposed as a temporary measure for 2022 to enable the use of food vouchers under art. No. 209, para. 1 of the Corporate Income Tax Act as a means of payment not only for food, but also for the repayment of bills used for domestic purposes of electricity and water. As of 1 April 2022, the final tax on income from interest on bank accounts acquired by local natural persons has been proposed. Expenditure policies of budget 2022. The most significant measures in key sectors. It's income policy. For 2022, an increase in salaries and salaries of staff for election posts has been estimated. Following the presentation of the Bill, the President of the Committee on Budget and Finance Lyubomir Karimanski informed the members of the Commission that a proposal for the dismissal of §10 of the Transitional and Final Rules will be submitted between the first and second votes of the Bill. The aim is to suspend the vicious legislative practice of changing laws that have no direct connection to the basic bill and should be submitted to the National Assembly as separate bills. A discussion was held on the bill, which included the following opinions and requests: In response to the question of the National Representative Lyubomir Karimanski from "There is such a people" regarding the data gap in the macroeconomic forecast of the Ministry of Finance and the Bulgarian National Bank for the Development of the State Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of Finance Asen Vassilev pointed out that at the beginning of January 2022 the data of the two institutions were quite close, after which BNB revised some of its views by increasing the expected inflation and reducing the expected growth. In addition, the manager of BNB Dimitar Radev noted that there is really a gap in the time horizon of the different assumptions, but this difference should not be institutionalized. BNB's expectations are the effect of investments in Bulgaria to appear stronger next year, where BNB predicts growth 4.5%, while the forecast of the Ministry of Finance is 3.7%. As for inflation in 2021, there is low inflation at the beginning of the year and a strong rise at the end of the year, as a result of which average annual inflation is relatively low. This inflation dynamics will be reproduced with a reverse sign in 2022, with average annual inflation in 2022 higher than 2021 due to high inflation at the beginning of the year. People's representative Georgi Ganev from "Democratic Bulgaria" supported the Bill, stressing that the deficit at stake is due to ambitious capital expenditure. According to him, it can be expected to over-execute the revenue and default of the expenditure, which will lead to the lack of deficit and therefore to new debt. It concludes from this that the budget is not very tense, there are reserves in both revenue and expenditure. The opinion of the MPs Kiril Ananiev, Krasimir Valchev, Irena Dimova and Alexander Ivanov from GERB-SDS is that the presented budget of economic growth and social policy is highly overrated and optimistic, given that for 2021 capital expenditure of 6 100 million is foreseen. BGN, and only 3,700 million are realized. lv. The investment process is a specific and slow process, and if there are no projects ready, the government's desired boost of GDP growth will hardly be achieved. According to them, this is the debt budget, as debt will reach high levels of about 50 billion. lv. or 29.3% of GDP. For them this is a pro-inflation budget, which will lead to an increase in inflation and hence the impoverishment of Bulgarian citizens. They pointed out that there is a serious risk to the financial stability of the country and there is a risk for Bulgaria's entry into the euro area. There is no provision for an increase in the remuneration of lecturers in higher schools and for the Bulgarian Academy of Sciences. People's Representative Bajram Bajram of the Law and expressed his position that the budget may be pro-inflationary. People's representative Tsoncho Ganev of AGE expressed strong conviction that the capital expenditures thus invested will not be realised, as companies will give up due to high inflation. According to him, the funds that he has placed are a buffer that will be used in the update of the budget. People's representative Professor Rumen Gechev from BSP for Bulgaria (BSP) defended the presented budget, stressing that worries about high deficit and threat of the country's fiscal stability are unfounded, given the deficits of other European countries, such as France, Italy, Romania, Hungary and the Czech Republic. People's Representative Andrey Gurov of the European Union Continued The Change has expressed support for the budget as the budget implements all commitments made in the coalition agreement. It's a budget of change in thinking. On the one hand, the most vulnerable populations in times of crisis are supported, and on the other, growth is promoted through targeted investments that will not lead to a disruption of the financial stability of the state in view of the deficits set by other European countries. In response to the concerns expressed above by the MPs, the Deputy Prime Minister and Minister of Finance Asen Vassilev noted that the debt contained in the previous government's medium-term forecast is much higher than the debt invested in the current forecast for 2021. The pledged debt is 25.6% of GDP and the real debt is 23.9% of GDP; in 2022 the proposed debt of the previous government is 27.2% of GDP and the current government offers 24.8% of GDP. Therefore, there is a much more cost-effective part invested in the Bulgarian economy, but with less debt. With regard to inflation, there will be clarity around the middle of the year in view of the policies of the European Central Bank, as Bulgaria does not pursue its own monetary policy. The budget is absolutely social because, however large inflation is, the funds that are directed towards vulnerable groups are much more than inflation can eat. As regards the implementation of the capital programme, this depends entirely on the relevant portfolio ministries that are ready for projects. In addition, from 8.2 billion. lv. capital expenditure is foreseen under the Recovery and Sustainability Plan to come to 1.6 billion. BGN, i.e. 6.6 billion lv. are national funds. Regarding the financial stability of the state Minister Vassilev pointed out that for 20 years a policy of fiscal conservatism has been conducted, which, at his discretion, is the reason for Bulgaria to be left behind from all other Eastern European countries. According to him, in order to become a poor country rich, it is necessary: - to increase the ratio of capital to labour, i.e. to have investment in capital infrastructure and machinery, so that people become more productive; - to increase people's qualifications in order to become more productive; - to improve the institutional framework so that capital and labour face less administrative difficulties, no corruption, a normal judicial system with a fast and fair administration based on the Law. Regarding the expenditure of the central budget Minister Vassilev pointed out that they are clearly and accurately scheduled in art. 1, para. 5 of the State Budget Bill 2022 The Governor of the Bulgarian National Bank Dimitar Radev expressed the principle position of the bank for a more conservative budget. As signed, the current budget envisions fiscal expansion in 2022 and limited fiscal consolidation over the next three years. Such a policy poses serious risks, but still depends on the implementation of the budget. Given the capacity of the Ministry of Finance, its expectations are that there will eventually be a more disciplined budget. The manager stressed that we are in a very unusual year characterized by a continuous budget cycle, but at the same time the budget is being implemented. The Chairman of the Fiscal Council, Boris Grozdanov, stated that the Fiscal Council agreed without objections the Bill because the Ministry of Finance successfully benefited from the derogation of fiscal rules and the exceptional situation related to the pandemic and managed to strengthen the social function of the budget. The Ombudsman of the Republic of Bulgaria Diana Kovacheva urged the MPs to introduce a mechanism for updating the amount of tax benefits under art. 18 of the Law on Income Tax on Individuals for Disabled Workers, as well as to include military invalids within the scope of the Law on Persons with Disabilities. The President of the National Association of Municipalities Silvia Georgieva expressed satisfaction with the increased capital subsidy for municipalities by 30% and the matching by 15%. It proposed to consider setting a buffer in the central budget to cover the deficits of municipalities for energy carriers and for construction materials, as has been done for water companies. He also expressed hope to return the text on financial corrections, as well as to provide for more funds to increase the remuneration of employees in municipal administrations. Deputy Chairman of the Bulgarian Industrial Association Maria Mincheva voiced concerns regarding macroeconomic assumptions and, above all, about inflation. It expressed support with regard to the proposed priorities with a focus on investment, the continuation of measures to support employment and compensate non-bit electricity consumers and the increase in the maximum amount of vouchers and the possibility of paying electricity and water bills. As far as increased social costs are concerned, it is necessary to have an advance growth in economic growth, not vice versa, because a good business environment can provide the basis for such payments. The Chairman of the Confederation of Independent Trade Unions in Bulgaria Plamen Dimitrov expressed his support for the budget for both the investment programme and the expenditure in the social sphere. The President of BESKO Dobromir Ivanov noted that although there are no reforms in the state budget for 2022, he strongly hopes they will be laid down in the update of the budget in July 2022. The Chairman of the Bulgarian Academy of Sciences Julian Revalski urged the MPs to consider between first and second reading of the Bill higher budget funding of science, as maintaining scientific potential in a high-level country is part of its sovereignty. The Chairman of the Branch Chamber of Taxi Drivers and Carriers Spas Atanasov and Krasimir Tsvetkov, called on the MPs to consider the possibility to set compensation for the municipalities of the state budget for the difference in the amount of the annual tax on the taxi transport of passengers for 2022 and the minimum annual amount of the tax under art. 61f of the Law on Local Taxes and Fees of 300 BGN. The Standing Committees of the Forty-Seventh National Assembly at their meetings have discussed, discussed and expressed their support under the State Budget Bill of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022. After the discussion was concluded, a vote was held and the following results were obtained: 1. Relying on low wages and low incomes as a competitive advantage must remain in the past, because there is no way that our competitive advantage is that we are poor and low incomes. If we sell this, becoming rich, we eat our competitive advantage. Our competitive advantage should be that we produce products with high added value, that there is sufficient mechanization of processes so that there is much more productivity of work, and that in the country we can live easily, move quickly and not waste our time. From this point of view, the main parameters of the budget bet a double increase in capital investments to 5.8% of GDP, wagering a significant increase and investment in funds in education, in health, also in culture and sport, wagering a social programme to ensure that there is social solidarity for our fellow citizens. If we have to talk in specific numbers, really with four billion debt, four and a half last year, and this year we have an increase in spending by about 12 billion. That is, eight billion increases in costs are not made at the expense of debt, but are made at the expense of additional receipts in the treasury. This means that, on the one hand, revenue agencies have done their job. Last year, although for seven months, this year is also an increase in the collection rate, and on the other hand, it means that Bulgarian companies and workers have managed to manufacture this product. The inflation in the budget is 5.6% on an annual average basis. We expect it to be higher in the first half of the year because the levels were lower last year, in the second half of the year it to be lower because we have already seen inflation processes from October, November, December, quite high in 2021, so 2022 compared to 2021 will not be so high. If this is not justified, it will accordingly be reflected in macroeconomic forecasts and in the update of the budget in June. The debt, which is scheduled for next year as a percentage of gross domestic product, is also significantly lower. For next year it was envisaged 27.2% in the mid-term forecast, we predict 24.8%. So this budget, despite significant additional investments, is actually being carried out with less debt than the budgets that were foreseen last year. And this is extremely important to be told to Bulgarian citizens. And when colleagues from GERB ask: where are the additional revenues, where are the eight billion, where are the stopped taps here. We make investments of 12 billion more, with debt that is less than what you have planned. Thank you. Yes, at that point what the country needed was discipline because it spent more than money. So far, so good. We are entering the European Union. We have one of the lowest debts to gross domestic product, we have surpluses. What is Bulgaria doing at this time as an economic policy? It's still the old one! It does not invest in people, it does not invest in infrastructure, it does not reduce the state administration, it increases the state administration in a period when it is reduced and switched to electronic services worldwide. And the result is that over half a million Bulgarians leave the country and go to work abroad, because there the working conditions are better, there are better investments, there are more capital, they are more productive and therefore receive more income. That is, since 2007 this economic policy of non-investment, beyond what comes from Europe, leads to half a million Bulgarians leaving the country and going to work abroad and not here, because here the public environment, the capital environment, the investment environment do not allow them to protect the income they can protect abroad. And this is the main difference between this budget and all budgets so far. This budget wants to make in Bulgaria the investment environment and public environment at least as good as it is in Europe. [[Applause from "We are continuing the Change."]] Not to put up with the fact that we are poor and will remain poor forever. Because the ratio is what determines whether we can repay this debt, or not be able to repay it. Regardless of what you have written as absolute values of gross domestic product and absolute values of debt, you have assumed that a higher ratio of debt to gross domestic product is sustainable. And what we're saying is that our ratio is lower, that is, in no way can we talk about a debt spiral or endangering the foundations of the economy, or anything else. The ratio we bet is lower than the ratio you have bet, which means that the state will be able to repay this debt on the basis of the gross domestic product it has achieved. But more importantly, when this debt is taken and invested, it will lead to more gross domestic product and higher incomes. ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-10.u370	Dear colleagues! ♪ I can't believe for the first vote on bills amending and supplementing the Law on Measures and Actions during the Emergency, announced by the National Assembly Decision of 13 March 2020, and for overcoming the consequences: No 47-102-01-6, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 6 December 2021, and No 47-154-01-5, submitted by Nikolina Angelkova and Desislava Atanasova on 17 December 2021. The Committee on Tourism at its meeting, held on 27 January 2022, examined and discussed in the first vote two draft laws amending and supplementing the Law on Measures and Actions during the emergency situation announced by a decision of the National Assembly of 13 March 2020 and to address the consequences: Draft Law No 47-102-01-6, submitted by the Council of Ministers. Draft Law No 47-154-01-5, submitted by Nikolina Angelkova and Desislava Atanasova. The meeting was attended by the Ministry of Tourism: Mrs. Irena Georgieva Deputy Minister and Mrs. Maya Nikovska Director of the Tourism Policy Directorate. The motives of the bill were presented by Mrs. Irena Georgieva. She pointed out that the proposed amendment restores the provision of art. 26, para. 1 of the Law on Measures and Actions during the Emergency, announced by a decision of the National Assembly of 13 March 2020, and to overcome the consequences before the cancellation of the last supplement in the Official Journal (bg. 67 from 2021) . This will restore the legal basis on the basis of which Scheme SA.58050 has been notified, namely State aid to tour operators using air carriers with a valid operating licence for the execution of charter flights to the Republic of Bulgaria for tourism purposes (SA.58050 (2020/N) approved by the Commission by Decision C2020) 5207 of 24 July 2020. The Ministry of Tourism argued that, after consulting all interested parties in October 2021, the scheme with the amended requirement for the use of Bulgarian air carriers with a valid operating licence was decided not to apply. The European Commission is informed that a proposal from the Council of Ministers is envisaged to be submitted to the Forty-Seventh National Assembly of Legislative Change, which will cancel the text of art. 26, para. 1 promulgated on 16 September 2021 and restored the text of the measure before the last amendment. In addition, when extending the Temporary Framework, the aid scheme SA.58050 will be extended through a group notification. The Ministry of Tourism has informed the European Commission that the scheme in this amended version has been proposed by the Committee on Economic Policy, Innovation and Tourism at the Forty-sixth National Assembly and the Ministry of Tourism has not been informed of the envisaged amendments to the Law on the State Budget of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2021 until the official vote of the National Assembly on its adoption. With the earlier changes that led to the suspension of the measure, the word "Bulgarian" was added to the air carriers with a valid operating license for the implementation of chartered and regular flights to the Republic of Bulgaria for tourism purposes, a circumstance which is assessed by the European Commission as a discriminatory text to the other air carriers. At the same time, it was admitted to the aid for the same seat to apply both tour operators and air carriers. The Ministry of Tourism confirmed that, after consulting all interested parties in October 2021, the scheme with the amended requirement for the use of Bulgarian air carriers with a valid operating licence was decided not to apply. The Commission is informed that a proposal from the Council of Ministers is to be submitted to the Forty-Seventh National Assembly of Legislative Change, which will cancel the text of art. 26, para. 1 promulgated on 16 September 2021 and restored the text of the measure before the last amendment. In addition, when extending the temporary framework, the aid scheme SA.58050 will be extended through a group notification. The amendment of the norm shall be aimed at the State aid under art. 26 of the Bill to be continued retroactively, i.e. the reverse effect of the Law of 16 September 2021, without interruption the support of tour operators who, during the period from 1 June 2021 to 31 December 2021, provided tourist packages to end-users Bulgaria. The objective of the scheme, which is popular as the EUR 35 per seat, is also to mitigate the socio-economic consequences for the tourism sector of the CoVID-19 pandemic by ensuring that the recipients of State aid can recover from the crisis and stabilise their liquidity. Ms. Georgieva pointed out that the draft proposed supplement in § 2 related to the possibility of applying vaccines against COVID-19 to the persons referred to in the provision and in 2022. It constitutes an effective measure in line with the policy to promote vaccination. With the adoption of the proposal, further protection of the outlined contingent will be ensured in the fight against COVID-19. On behalf of the importers under Law No 47-154-01-5 of 17 December 2021, the amendments thereto presented Ms. Nikolina Angelkova. She noted that the tourism sector not only suffers losses but is facing the edge of survival and there is no time for more wrong solutions, and the negotiation of charter programmes from the key markets for Bulgaria is of great importance for the restoration of Bulgarian tourism. The proposal shall return the text of the provision prior to the amendment of the Law on Measures and Actions during the emergency situation announced by a decision of the National Assembly of 13 March 2020 and to address the consequences. When discussing the two draft laws, the following differences were found: the duration of the measure in the Law of the Council of Ministers is two years and aims to continue retroactively to avoid interruption of the support of tour operators who, during the period from 1 June 2021 to 31 December 2021, have provided tourist packages to end-users Bulgaria, while the national representative Nikolina Angelkova and the national representative Desislava Atanasova propose that this period be from the entry into force of the law and to continue three years after the emergency situation has been lifted. Also in Law No 47-154-01-5 of 17 December 2021, submitted by the MPs Angelkova and Atanasova, there is no regulation for extension and in 2022 of the period for the application of vaccines against COVID-19 of the persons outlined in the provision. Participants in the debates on the two drafts took part in the discussion: Ilin Dimitrov, Nikolina Angelkova, Ivaylo Hristov, Krasen Kralev, Dimitar Tashev, Georgi Svilenski and Vasil Zashkev. After the discussion, the two bills were put to the vote: - under Law No 47-154-01-5, submitted by Nikolina Angelkova and Desislava Atanasova on 17 December 2021, the result was 3 The Commission does not support the proposal. - under Law No 47-102-01-6, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 6 December 2021, the result was 10.................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................. The Commission supports the proposal. On the basis of the presented drafts and in the light of the votes carried out, the Committee on Tourism proposed to the National Assembly to adopt the Draft Law No 47-102-01-6, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 6 December 2021.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-10.u372	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen of the House ♪ I can't believe on a Draft Law amending and supplementing the Law on Measures and Actions during the Emergency, announced by the National Assembly Decision of 13 March 2020, No 47-102-01-6, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 6 December 2021, and a Draft Law amending and supplementing the Law on Measures and Actions during the Emergency, announced by the National Assembly Decision of 13 March 2020, No 47-154-01-5, submitted by the National Representative Nikolina Angelkova and the National Representative Desislava Atanasova on 17 December 2021. At a meeting held on 27 January 2022, the Budget and Finance Committee examined those bills. The meeting was attended by: Mrs. Irena Georgieva from the Ministry of Tourism, Deputy Minister and Mrs. Maya Nikovska from the Ministry of Tourism. The bill amending and supplementing the Law on Measures and Actions during the Emergency, announced by the National Assembly Decision of 13 March 2020, No 47-102-01-6, was presented by Mrs. Irena Georgieva Deputy Minister. The proposed amendment aims to restore the provision of art. 26, para.1 of the Law on Measures and Actions during the Emergency, announced by decision of the National Assembly of 13 March 2020, prior to the change from the last addition to the Official Journal, No. 77 of 2021. Thus, the legal basis on which the Scheme SA.58050 was notified will be restored, namely: the State aid to tour operators using air carriers with a valid operating licence for the implementation of charter flights to the Republic of Bulgaria for tourism, approved by the Commission by Decision C (2020) 5207 of 24 July 2020. With the change of the norm is expected through the State aid under art. 26 of the Bill to continue the support of tour operators who have provided tourist packages to consumers with a destination point of arrival the territory of the Republic of Bulgaria. In addition to tour operators, charter flights, the measure can also benefit those of them who have used places in regular flights or flights of low-cost airlines to bring tourists to Bulgaria. The proposed supplement in § 2 of the Draft Law relating to the possibility of applying vaccines against COVID-19 to the persons outlined in the provision constitutes an effective measure consistent with the policy to promote vaccination. Given the existence of sufficient amounts of vaccines in the country for the administration of a third booster dose of the fully vaccinated persons and the presence of persons in those groups who have not yet completed their complete vaccination, the possibility that all of them may receive a vaccine against COVID-19 in 2022 should not be discontinued due to the expiry of calendar year 2021. This measure does not impose an additional budget. The bill amending and supplementing the Law on Measures and Actions during the Emergency, announced by decision of the National Assembly of 13 March 2020, No 47-154-01-5, was presented by Ms. Nikolina Angelkova. The proposed bill is identical to that tabled by the Council of Ministers. The proposal shall return the text of the provision prior to the last amendment to the Law on Measures and Actions during the emergency situation announced by the National Assembly's decision of 13 March 2020 and to address the consequences. The previous norm was adopted in May 2020 as a measure to assist the tourism sector in tackling the effects of the crisis. It is key to continue to function with the old version by erasing the words "Bulgarian" in front of the air carriers in the text of the current article. 26, para. 1 of the Law. According to the importer of the Bill, by changing the provision, it will avoid violating the requirements for free competition in the internal European market, as well as discriminatory treatment with regard to air carriers operating on the free European market. It is proposed that this extremely important measure for the tourism sector be used three years after the emergency situation has been lifted and to support the recovery of the sector, which, according to the World Tourism Organisation, is expected in 2023. During the discussion the President of the Committee on Budget and Finance expressed the opinion that a preliminary impact assessment should be applied to the Draft Law submitted by the Council of Ministers, as well as an analysis of the costs-benefits to the Law submitted by the National Representative Nikolina Angelkova and the National Representative Desislava Atanasova in order to know the effect of the implementation of this measure. Ms. Irena Georgieva replied that no impact assessment was applied in the documents submitted to the Council of Ministers, referring to § 1a. 4 of the Law on Legislative Acts, as this is a project related to preventing and eliminating the effects of force majeure. For the next meeting, the Ministry of Tourism undertook to provide an analysis of the situation with quantitative parameters in order to clarify the importance of this measure. What should be noted is that the decline in tourist visits for 2020 compared to 2019 is about 80%. According to the Ministry of Tourism, the subsidy received it 507 flights for a total of 58 246 tourists and 26 tour operators. In the event that the measure is implemented by the end of 2022, this will be stimulating for this sector of the economy, such as the funds for it in the budget, which is around 12'13 million. BGN, are foreseen. Mr. Tsoncho Ganev said that according to him, the text of the Draft Law submitted by the Council of Ministers should be precise by stating that the measure is applicable to no less than 70 seats. Its second proposal concerns the provision for the measure to be applied also to the tourist accommodation by bus and coach transport. Mr. Lyubomir Karimanski said that the issue raised about bus companies concerns another measure and Mrs. Nikolina Angelkova clarified that if we extend the scope of the measure beyond the one approved by the European Commission, we risk a new notification. The Ministry of Tourism commented that it is very difficult to detect the number of tourists by bus, it can be thought in this direction, but if the measure proposed by the Law is not accepted, it will be a serious blow to our country's image. After the discussion was concluded, a vote was held and the following results were obtained: 1. ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-10.u494	Mr. Chairman, ladies and gentlemen! ♪ I can't believe on a bill amending the Investment Promotion Act, No 47-254-01-7, submitted by Petar Nikolaev Kulenski and a group of MPs on 19 January 2022. At its regular meeting on 26 January 2022, the Committee on Economic Policy and Innovation examined the Bill amending the Investment Promotion Act. The bill presented by the National Representative Petar Kulenski was drawn up in connection with the Decision of the Council of Ministers No 892 of 30 December 2021, which shows that the Bulgarian Investment Agency is moving to the Ministry of Innovation and Growth and that the Minister of Innovation and Growth exercises the rights of the state in the capital of the Bulgarian Development Bank. This imposes in the Investment Promotion Act, the Bulgarian Development Bank Act, the Bulgarian Citizenship Act and the Foreigners Act in the Republic of Bulgaria the words The bill complies with the requirements of the Law on Legislative Acts, the Decree on its implementation and of the Rules on Organization and Activity of the National Assembly. In their written opinion of the Ministry of Innovation and Growth, they support the bill. During the discussion the MPs Alexander Ivanov and Petar Kanev expressed reservations about the chosen approach for legislative initiative. National Representative Desislava Trifonova asked whether the establishment rules of the Ministry are ready as a mandatory condition for his work and at what stage is the preparation of the regulation under art. 14, para. 4 of the Law on Bulgarian Citizenship, which will regulate the control of investment citizenship. In response, Karina Angelievka, Deputy Minister of Innovation and Growth, pointed out that the Ministry's rules of procedure are at the stage of public discussion and after its adoption will begin the work on the regulation as well. After the presentation and discussion of the bill, a vote was held, which ended with the following results: for 12, without objection, and the abstention of 7 Members of the National Assembly. On the basis of the above, the Committee on Economic Policy and Innovation proposes to the National Assembly to adopt a bill amending the Investment Promotion Act, No 47-254-01-7, submitted by Petar Nikolaev Kulenski and a group of MPs on 19 January 2022.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-10.u784	Mr. Chairman, ladies and gentlemen of the House! ♪ I can't believe on a bill amending and supplementing the Financial Instruments Markets Act, No 47-102-01-8, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 10 December 2021. At a meeting held on 20 January 2022, the Budget and Finance Committee examined that bill. The meeting was attended by representatives of the Ministry of Finance: Kaloyan Simeonov, co-ordinating director of the Department of Financial Markets and Elena Petrova, Head of Department of the Directorate of Financial Markets and Nadia Daskalova, State Expert in the Directorate of Financial Markets, representatives of the Financial Supervision Commission, the Association of Bulgarian insurers and the Bulgarian Association of Licensed Investment Mediators. The draft is intended to introduce in national law the requirements of Directive (EU) 2019/2034 on prudential supervision of investment firms as well as measures implementing Regulation (EU) 2019/2033 on prudential requirements for investment firms, Directive (EU) 2019/878 amending Directive 2013/36/EU as regards exempted entities, financial holding companies, mixed financial holding companies, remuneration, supervisory measures and powers and Directive (EU) 2019/2177 amending Directive 2009/138/EC, Directive 2014/65/EU and Directive (EU) 2015/849. The amendments resulting from the introduction of the package of Directive (EU) 2019/2034 and Regulation (EU) 2019/2033 concern the introduction of a proportionate and harmonised regulatory regime on prudential requirements for investment firms. In essence, the main change to the current prudential framework consists in the calculation of own funds requirements for investment firms depending on the risks they take. Investment firms shall be classified informally in 3 classes and class 2 and 3 shall fall under the powers of the Financial Supervision Commission. The bill introduces an obligation for so-called system investment firms [[or also referred to as Class 1 with assets over 30 billion. EUR]] apply to the Bulgarian National Bank for a credit institution licence under the Credit Institutions Act. There is also a procedure for re-licensing an investment firm licence by the Financial Supervision Commission in cases where those system intermediaries no longer meet the requirements and their assets fall below the threshold of 30 billion. Euros. Currently, there are no class 1 intermediaries in Bulgaria. The amendments resulting from the introduction of Directive (EU) 2019/878 concern the introduction of a specific approval procedure and direct supervisory powers of the competent authorities with regard to certain financial holding companies and mixed holding companies in order to ensure that those holding companies can be directly responsible for ensuring compliance with prudential requirements on a consolidated basis without subjecting them to additional prudential requirements on an individual basis. The amendments resulting from the introduction of Directive (EU) 2019/2177 refer to the fact that the licensing functions of data reporting services providers and the supervision of data collection powers are transferred by national competent authorities to the European Securities and Markets Regulatory Authority, with the exception of those which could benefit from the derogation provided for in Regulation (EU) No 600/2014 due to their limited importance for the internal market of the European Union. The Bulgarian Stock Exchange is a data reporting services provider that will be able to benefit from the derogation and continue to report to the Financial Supervision Commission. The Bill also provides for other amendments arising from European legislation. The transitional and final provisions in the Insurance Code relating to the publication in the Official Journal of the EU of a notice on the adjustment to take account of inflation of the amounts provided for in Directive 2009/138/EC relating to the start and pursuit of the insurance and reinsurance business published on 19 October 2021 should apply from 19 October 2022 by the Member States. The bill complies with the requirements of the Law on Regulatory Acts, the Decree on its implementation and of the Rules on Organization and Activity of the National Assembly. The expected effects of the application of the future law are that the envisaged changes will lead to a change in the methodology by which investment firms that are not systematically significant calculate their own capital requirements. In addition, this will lead to an increase in the administrative burden and costs for investment firms and C.F.R.S. relating to initial compliance costs with new prudential requirements. At the same time, however, with the introduction of a regime that is simpler and proportionate to the risks that investment firms take with their activities, it is expected to achieve a reduction in the running costs of intermediaries to comply with regulatory requirements. During the discussion, the People's Representative Kiril Ananiev noted that the bill corresponds to the directives and the group of GERB-SDS will support it. Professor Rumen Gechev also said that his group will support the bill. In relation to the reasons for the Bill and the envisaged obligation for the FSC to regularly review at least every three years for the compliance of investment firms with the requirements for the use of internal models, he asked what mechanisms exist for guaranteeing buyers on the Bulgarian market, whether the checks that the FSC must carry out at least once every 3 years for investment firms are sufficient and what is the risk in carrying out supervision. The C.F.R. has indicated that, pursuant to Directive (EU) 2019/2034 and Regulation (EU) 2019/2033, the regular review requirement of 3 years only relates to the verification by the C.F.R.S. in cases where investment firms have been authorised to use internal models. Under the current law, investment firms are now able to apply internal models, but at present no one has benefited from this and have applied the standard approach set out in Regulation (EU) 2019/2033. The President of the Committee on Budget and Finance Lyubomir Karimanski expressed concern that if the bill is not adopted soon, Bulgaria will tolerate sanctions, which, according to the European Commission Communication from 2021/C 2283 of 12 April 2021, will be 622 000 thousand. Euros. In this regard, the representative of the Ministry of Finance stated that, by the end of January 2022, an answer should be sent to the Commission on how far we have come with the introduction of European legislation, which also applies to the drafting of regulations. Ms. Maria Filipova pointed out that a draft regulation for amendment and supplement to Ordinance No 50 of 2015 on capital adequacy, liquidity of investment firms and supervision of their compliance, for which the public discussion has passed and the proposals and opinions received are currently under consideration. People's representative Plamen Shalfov asked a question about what are the benefits for investment firms or consumers of their products. In his reply Mr. Kaloyan Simeonov stressed that the amendments provided for in the bill will lead to a change in the methodology by which investment firms, which are not systematically significant, will calculate their capital requirements. Ms. Nina Kolchakova from the Bulgarian Insurance Association expressed concern regarding the cancellation of para. 5 of art. 493a of the Insurance Code, stating that between the first and second votes of the Bill will deposit their written opinion on the subject. After the discussion was concluded, a vote was held and the following results were obtained: 13 votes................................. and 0 votes.......................................................................... On the basis of the above and the results of the vote, the Committee on Budget and Finance proposes to the National Assembly to adopt a Draft for amending and supplementing the Financial Instruments Markets Act, No 47-102-01-8, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 10 December 2021.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-10.u817	Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members! At its regular meeting held on 3 February 2022, the Environment and Water Commission discussed that bill. The meeting was attended by: the Ministry of Environment and Water: Mr. Borislav Sandov, Deputy Prime Minister for Climate Policies and Minister for Environment and Water; Mrs. Maria Achkova, Deputy Minister for Environment and Water, and representatives of the Bulgarian Industrial Association and the Association for Research Practices. On behalf of the importers, the bill and the reasons for it were presented by Minister Sandov. The aim of the Bill is to overcome the existing regulatory gap in terms of the duration of river basin management plans and flood risk management plans. The proposed changes will regulate the implementation of the adopted plans until their update. At the moment, they expired on 31 December 2021. As of 1 January 2022, the next planning period The delay is due to their adoption for the previous period 2016 " 2021," with one year's delay, at the end of 2016. The untimely update of the plans may lead to a flood risk due to the failure to implement measures. In addition, there will be a time period during which procedures for issuing, amending and/or continuing authorisations for the use of water will be difficult to carry out, as well as advice on eligibility of investment intentions. The proposed changes will ensure the timely procedure under the Water Act, the Environmental Protection Act and the Biodiversity Act. Conditions will be created for the implementation of business initiatives and administrations related to water and water sites, which should be implemented in accordance with existing management plans. The bill complies with the requirements of the Law on Legislative Acts, the Decree on its implementation and of the Rules on Organization and Activity of the National Assembly. Representatives of all political parties stressed that they would support the bill and united around the opinion that it should be done as quickly as possible. ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-10.u895	Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members! ♪ I can't believe on a bill amending and supplementing the Consumer Protection Act No 47-102-01-2 submitted by the Council of Ministers on 3 December 2021. At its regular meeting on 19 January 2022, the Committee on Economic Policy and Innovation examined the Draft Law amending and supplementing the Consumer Protection Act. The draft, submitted by the Deputy Minister of Economy and Industry Dimitar Danchev, aims to introduce in national legislation the requirements of Directive 2019/2161 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 November 2019 amending Council Directive 93/13/EEC and Directives 98/6/EC, 2005/29/EC and 2011/83/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council as regards the better implementation and modernisation of consumer protection rules in the Union. Individual remedies are provided for consumers who have suffered damage from the application of unfair trade practices by traders, enabling consumers to claim a price reduction or compensation. The criteria on which the Commission for Consumer Protection will assess when a commercial practice is unfair, for example, any supply of goods in a Member State as identical to a commodity marketed in other Member States, where that commodity has significant differences in composition or characteristics, unless justified by legal and objective factors. Criteria are proposed for the imposition of fines and property penalties, taking into account the nature, gravity, scale, duration and frequency of the infringement, as well as any means of protection granted by the trader to consumers for the damage caused, financial benefits and losses avoided. The bill provides for an increase in the amount of fines and penalty payments for infringements of the provisions of the Consumer Protection Act, especially with regard to the widespread infringements and the widespread infringements with a Union dimension, which are subject to measures for coordinated enforcement activities under Regulation 2017/2394. The property penalty for a widespread infringement with a Union dimension is up to 4% of the turnover realised by the trader for the previous financial year in the Member State concerned or in each of the Member States participating in the coordinated activity and, where there is no information on the turnover, a penalty of three to 920 000 BGN will be imposed. The scope of the Consumer Protection Act is extended so as to cover the provision of free-of-charge content [[digital content provided against personal data]] and digital services to ensure the protection of users of digital content and digital services in distance contracts and off-premises. The requirements for online trading venues are regulated and specific requirements are laid down for the provision of information to consumers before being bound by a contract proposal and for the provision of information with regard to unfair trade practices and contracts concluded at a distance and off-premises in order to reduce the regulatory burden on businesses. Control of the implementation of the Law shall be entrusted to the Commission for Consumer Protection. The amendments envisaged do not lead to the need for additional funds and will have no impact on the state budget. The bill complies with the requirements of the Law on Legislative Acts, the Decree on its implementation and of the Rules on Organization and Activity of the National Assembly. In his written opinion of the Association, Food and Beverage Bulgaria is expressing disagreement with the provision of art. 190n, para. 3 and 4 and, in particular, with the possibility of the same infringement, that the trader should be punished twice and more times and a penalty on the turnover of all related parties should be imposed for the infringement. During the public discussion, Yana Ivanova, Executive Director of the Association, argued that their request to drop those texts, stressing that they contradict European law and empower the Commission for Consumer Protection to impose sanctions on the basis of turnover of companies that have not committed any infringement and for activities outside the territory of the Republic of Bulgaria. In response by the Ministry of Economy and Industry stressed that a fine-tuning of the text of para. 3 so that it is clear that the penalty is up to 4% on turnover realised in the countries affected by the infringement. During the discussion Prof. Rumen Gechev expressed his conviction that the interests of Bulgarian consumers should be protected regardless of the severity of the sanction, which should have a disciplinary effect, and asked how the punishing authority will calculate the amount of the punishment. In response, the set criteria for this were fully specified. After the presentation and discussion of the bill, a vote was held, which ended with the following results:....................... 11............................................................................................................................................................................................................................. On the basis of the above, the Committee on Economic Policy and Innovation proposes to the National Assembly to adopt in the first vote a bill amending and supplementing the Law on Consumer Protection, No 47-102-01-2, submitted by the Council of Ministers on 3 December 2021.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-10.u898	Thank you, Mrs. Dimitrusheva. Report from the Commission on Electronic Governance and Information Technology. Welcome, Mr. Simeonov.
ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-10.u899	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. The Committee on Electronic Governance and Information Technologies at a regular meeting held on 19 January 2022 discussed and discussed the Draft Law amending and supplementing the Consumer Protection Act submitted by the Council of Ministers. The meeting was attended by: Bozhidar Bojanov, Minister of Electronic Governance; Dimitar Danchev, Deputy Minister of Economy and Industry, Emil Aleksiev, Head of the Department of Consumer Politics, Department of Technical Harmonization and Consumer Policy.The Ministry of Economy and Industry; Dimitar Margaritov, Chairman of the Commission for Consumer Protection, Konstantin Raykov, Konstantin Arabadzhiev, Members of the Commission, and Ivan Dimitrov, Chairman of the Communications Regulation Commission. The bill was presented by the Deputy Minister of Economy and Industry Dimitar Danchev. In the course of the discussion, the Law amending and supplementing the Law on Consumer Protection was supported by the Minister of Electronic Governance, the President of the Commission for Consumer Protection and the President of the Communications Regulation Commission. Members of parliament Lyubomir Karimanski, Rosen Zhelyazkov and Kiril Simeonov took part in the discussion of the Law. They asked questions to the importers of the Bill and expressed their support. The Commission received the opinion of the Association "Food and Beverage Bulgaria." The Executive Director also took part in the discussion of the Bill. The Association supports the Bill and has specific proposals available to the Members of the National Assembly.
